| Agreement | On Option 1/2/3/4 for providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, capture the following in TR 38.769:<br>---<br>On the feasibility and applicability of Option 4 for providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, sources (LGE, Samsung) state that Option 4 is feasible and applicable after inventory procedure/registration.<br>- [LGE] Option 4 is feasible and considered applicable when the device completes the inventory procedure prior to the DO‑A data transmission.<br>- [Samsung] Study should include cases where a device autonomously triggers registration and/or data transmission. In practical deployments, a reader generally collects sensing data only from devices already associated/paired/registered with it, and requiring random access for each transmission would severely impact device energy consumption. Hence, enabling autonomous transmissions via device-specific resource configuration after registration (Option 4) is feasible and necessary.<br>On the other hand, sources (CATT, Lenovo, vivo) state that it is or may be infeasible.<br>- [CATT] Reader cannot know which device would require resource for DO-A transmission. Thus, it is very unlikely that Reader can provide the necessary information by device-specific R2D transmission.<br>- [Lenovo] Not support Option 4. In event-triggered resource allocation, resources are assigned only when a specific predefined event occurs - for example, when a device has DO-A data ready for transmission. Such allocation must be initiated through prior signaling, meaning it cannot be applied until after the first D2R transmission within the DO-A process. Consequently, this approach is not suitable for the very first D2R transmission of DO-A. Moreover, device-specific R2D transmissions are unavailable at this stage, so they cannot supply the necessary information for the first DO A D2R transmission.<br>- [vivo] For option4, if the first D2R transmission for DoA is provided by device-specific R2D transmission, it means that DoA traffic can only be served for a device after it is identified/inventoried by a reader, which is one kind of Do-DTT operation in fact. That is, option 4 is infeasible to support DoA traffic to some extent. For devices only oriented to DoA traffic, performing this device identification before the first D2R transmission of DoA traffic is also unnecessary, since it confers no concrete benefit and instead incurs unnecessary resource overhead and power consumption.<br>On Option 1/2/3/4 for providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, sources (Huawei, Spreadtrum, OPPO, Ericsson, CMCC, ETRI, DCM, Qualcomm, Quectel, Fraunhofer, vivo) support Option 1 based on the following observations.<br>- [Huawei] Broadcast information should be used to provide the necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A because it was agreed to be periodically transmitted, thereby ensuring that the device can receive this information regardless of the availability status of the device and the type of DO-A transmission (event-triggered or periodic) and guarantees the device to have the opportunity to transmit the first D2R transmission for DO-A. In the case of a two-step acquisition procedure, the necessary information is included in the SIB-like broadcast information, and the device can use the last received broadcast information to determine the necessary information.<br>- [OPPO] Candidate D2R resources for the first D2R transmission of DO-A traffic should be indicated by the broadcast information in the A-IoT SSB rather than paging like aperiodic R2D signal (i.e., Option 2). Because whether to transmit DO-A data is fully up to device, which is different from DT or DO-DTT where the device should transmit data whenever triggered by R2D. Thus, aperiodic D2R resource allocation for DO-A cannot timely adapt to the real DO-A transmission demand and will exacerbate the issue that the traffic of DO-A is available but lack of allocated D2R candidate resources.<br>- [Ericsson] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, the necessary information is provided by broadcast information.<br>- [CMCC] For Option 1, as long as device acquires cell synchronization and reads broadcast information, it can get the access resource allocation and initiate random access any time it has D2R traffic to report, this option is simple and more straightforward.<br>- [ETRI] From an initial DO-A transmission perspective, it is desirable for a device to obtain the required resource allocation and related parameters in a simple and deterministic manner, without relying on device-specific signaling or additional interaction with the reader. In this context, broadcast information naturally serves as a suitable mechanism for conveying such information. Providing the necessary information via broadcast information enables the device to acquire all required parameters after synchronization, without waiting for a paging-like message or performing additional decoding procedures. This approach is particularly beneficial for periodic DO-A transmission, where predictable transmission behavior and low latency are desired.<br>- [DCM] Necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A provided by periodic broadcast information should be considered.<br>- [Qualcomm] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, combining Option 1/3 with Option 2 is feasible, where Option 1/3 can be considered for periodic D2R resources and Option 2 can be used to adjust the D2R resources.<br>- [Quectel] The resource allocation information of the first D2R transmission for DO-A is included in paging-like message or broadcast information.<br>- [Fraunhofer] Select between Option 1 and Option 3 to provide necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A. Among the options under study for determining the initial set of resources for DO-A, Option 1 and 3 should be prioritized. These approaches not only minimize signaling overhead but also provides greater flexibility.<br>- [vivo] As agreed in RAN1, broadcast information is necessary for subsequent transmission and reception by all devices, so it is more signaling efficiency for periodic broadcast information to provide information of D2R transmission for both periodic DoA and event-triggered DoA traffics, compared to option2. For event-triggered DoA traffics, Option1 provide more guaranteed and stable DoA traffic service compared to option2 aperiodic paging-like triggering. Option 1 will not impact on the detection performance of periodic sync signal, compared to option3 that since necessary information derived from a synchronization signal e.g., by increase the number of sync signal sequences.<br>Following neutral or negative observations were also provided for Option 1.<br>- [LGE] Delivering the necessary information through broadcast information is simple and straightforward, but it increases signaling overhead when the broadcast information is transmitted together with a short‑period sync signal.<br>- [FUTUREWEI] Broadcast information contains at least one resource allocation. After a device obtains an ID for a DO-A group, the device can select the resource allocation associated with the group. This option offers the reader control to set the allocation globally.<br>- [ZTE] Option 1 is a periodic DO-A resource allocation scheme that provides predictable and reliable access opportunities with bounded access latency.<br>[Negative views]<br>- [CATT] For Options 1 and 3, the resource overhead and energy consumption are high with periodic transmission of the broadcast information. The A-IoT device also needs to store the information in non-volatile memory and count the time interval with the decoded broadcast information.<br>On Option 1/2/3/4 for providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, sources (CATT, Xiaomi, InterDigital, Qualcomm, Quectel, ASUSTeK) support Option 2 based on the following observations.<br>- [CATT] For Option 2‌, a paging-like indicator can be used as a polling message to query the A-IoT devices on the need of resource for first D2R transmission. This mechanism triggers device response to indicate whether DO-A message transmission is required.<br>- [Xiaomi] R2D message carrying DO-A indication information can be sent periodically. Between two adjacent R2D messages, several DO-A occasions can be pre-allocated by the adjacent R2D message. For the device who has DO-A data to transmit, it can choose one of the DO-A occasions to send DO-A transmission. For the DO-A occasion, it can be interpreted as a direct transmission occasion, or a transmission occasion for transmission request. For example, the R2D message can be paging, and the occasion is used for Msg1 transmission.<br>- [InterDigital] Since AIoT devices may have significant SFO, over long intervals, timing synchronization would be degraded. Therefore, to locate the resources accurately, a reference point such as a synchronization signal or a paging-like R2D message would be beneficial. A paging-like R2D message may incur higher overhead depending on the signal design but it would provide higher flexibility in resource allocation.<br>- [Qualcomm] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, combining Option 1/3 with Option 2 is feasible, where Option 1/3 can be considered for periodic D2R resources and Option 2 can be used to adjust the D2R resources. For Option 2: periodic broadcast information to indicate periodic monitoring occasions for paging message(s) and the paging message can indicate one-time resources for first D2R.<br>- [Quectel] The resource allocation information of the first D2R transmission for DO-A is included in paging-like message or broadcast information.<br>- [ASUSTeK] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, at least support Option 2 and Option 4. For the first D2R transmission autonomously triggered by active device, reader implements CBRA triggering which targets devices with pending DO-A data.<br>Following neutral or negative observations were also provided for Option 2.<br>- [FUTUREWEI] Paging-like information also contains at least one resource allocation. After a device obtains an ID for a DO-A group, the device can select the resource allocation associated with the group. This option offers the reader control to set the allocation globally.<br>- [ZTE] Option 2 supports periodic and aperiodic DO-A resource allocation. For the aperiodic allocation, the network can dynamically configure DO-A resources to enable more flexible scheduling and reducing both signaling and resource overhead. Through network coordination, Option 2 can effectively avoid resource conflicts between DO-A traffic and DT traffic and mitigate network congestion.<br>- [LGE] Delivering the necessary information through a paging‑like R2D message reduces signaling overhead at the reader side, but it introduces transmission delay because DO‑A transmission can only occur after the device receives the paging message.<br>[Negative views]<br>- [Huawei] If the necessary information is provided by a paging-like R2D message (Option 2), if this message is used for polling, there would be an increase in the transmission overhead due to the additional DO-A scheduling request and another paging message to provide resource allocation information for the first D2R transmission. If the message is intended to be triggered by the device, it is unclear how the device can do so without any resource allocation information for such a trigger to be transmitted.<br>- [CMCC] “Option2: necessary information provided by a paging-like R2D message” means more energy consumption before DOA traffic report than option 1 and has a risk of not enough energy for traffic report.<br>- [vivo] Since broadcast information is necessary for subsequent transmission and reception of all devices, if the necessary information is provided by the paging-like R2D message, additional signaling overhead and more power consumption will be caused for a device to get the first D2R resource, compared to option1. For periodic DoA traffic, using option2 aperiodic paging-like R2D message to trigger D2R resource of every periodicity will obviously cause signaling waste. For event-triggered DoA traffic, a reader cannot know when to do the first D2R resource scheduling via paging-like R2D message, thereby resulting in D2R transmission latency and maybe discontinuity of services.<br>- [ETRI] Paging-like R2D messages introduce additional signaling steps and may not be well suited for devices that have not yet established any access context.<br>On Option 1/2/3/4 for providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, sources (NEC, InterDigital, Sony, Qualcomm, Fraunhofer) support Option 3 based on the following observations.<br>- [NEC] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, Option 3 should be adopted as following: TR->D for time resource determination is pre-defined and the end of the corresponding R2D sync signal or the PRDCH carrying broadcast information is used for the device to determine the time resource for the first D2R transmission.<br>- [InterDigital] Since AIoT devices may have significant SFO, over long intervals, timing synchronization would be degraded. Therefore, to locate the resources accurately, a reference point such as a synchronization signal or a paging-like R2D message would be beneficial.<br>- [Sony] Option 3 has advantage over the others. When DO-A resources are conveyed via broadcast information, waiting for the next broadcast to detect changes may introduce unnecessary delays. Frequent monitoring of broadcast information to detect updates is inefficient for both devices and the system. Therefore, indicating updates to broadcast information via a field associated with a periodic synchronization signal can reduce unnecessary monitoring.<br>- [Qualcomm] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, combining Option 1/3 with Option 2 is feasible, where Option 1/3 can be considered for periodic D2R resources and Option 2 can be used to adjust the D2R resources.<br>- [Fraunhofer] Select between Option 1 and Option 3 to provide necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A. Among the options under study for determining the initial set of resources for DO-A, Option 1 and 3 should be prioritized. These approaches not only minimize signaling overhead but also provides greater flexibility.<br>Following neutral or negative observations were also provided for Option 3.<br>- [LGE] Delivering the resource information through synchronization signals is less desirable because blind detection of synchronization signals is required, which increases device complexity.<br>- [ZTE] Option 3 supports periodic and aperiodic DO-A resource allocation.<br>- [FUTUREWEI] This option can use MIB-like information and broadcast information. It combines control as well as allocation.<br>- [CMCC] Option 3 is still based on option 1, and details can be further studied.<br>[Negative views]<br>- [vivo] If the necessary information is derived by sync signal e.g., by increasing the number of sync signal sequences, it will have impact on the detection performance of sync signal. What necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A should be carried by periodic synchronization signal and also the corresponding benefit are unclear.<br>- [ETRI] Deriving information from synchronization signals may impose constraints on signal design and limit flexibility in conveying resource configuration details.<br>- [Huawei] If the necessary information is derived from a synchronization signal and provided by broadcast information (Option 3), it would require multiple sequences to be used to design the synchronization signal and the device to blindly detect these multiple sequences when performing the initial frequency acquisition procedure, which will add to the complexity and power consumption of the device.<br>- [CATT] For Options 1 and 3, the resource overhead and energy consumption are high with periodic transmission of the broadcast information. The A-IoT device also needs to store the information in non-volatile memory and count the time interval with the decoded broadcast information.<br>On Option 1/2/3/4 for providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A, source (ASUSTeK) supports Option 4.<br>- [ASUSTeK] For providing necessary information for the first D2R transmission for DO-A for Device 2b and for Device C, at least support Option 2 and Option 4. For the first D2R transmission autonomously triggered by active device, reader implements CBRA triggering which targets devices with pending DO-A data.<br>Following neutral or negative observations were also provided for Option 4.<br>- [ZTE] The DO-A traffic/resource periodicity is indicated to a connected device via the dedicated device-specific R2D transmission. The device then transmits its DO-A data payload directly based on the period. This option is appropriate for connected devices that have periodic DO-A reporting requirements. For example, when a connected device is configured to periodically report sensor data or status updates.<br>[Negative views]<br>- [Qualcomm] For Option 4: the first D2R transmission may be interpreted differently but the resource allocation for the first D2R transmission can be based on paging message in Option 2. If a device needs to go through inventory procedure before DO-A data reporting, the paging message with CBRA indication (as in Option 2) is used to trigger the inventory procedure (Msg1 is the first D2R), and after inventory procedure the device-specific R2D message can trigger DO-A reporting. If a device is known by the reader and does not need to go through inventory procedure, the paging message with CFA indication (as in Option 2) can be used to trigger the DO-A data reporting (DO-A data is the first DO-A).<br>- [vivo] For option4, if the first D2R transmission for DoA is provided by device-specific R2D transmission, it means that DoA traffic can only be served for a device after it is identified/inventoried by a reader, which is one kind of Do-DTT operation in fact. That is, option 4 is infeasible to support DoA traffic to some extent. For devices only oriented to DoA traffic, performing this device identification before the first D2R transmission of DoA traffic is also unnecessary, since it confers no concrete benefit and instead incurs unnecessary resource overhead and power consumption.<br>- [Lenovo] Not support Option 4. In event-triggered resource allocation, resources are assigned only when a specific predefined event occurs - for example, when a device has DO-A data ready for transmission. Such allocation must be initiated through prior signaling, meaning it cannot be applied until after the first D2R transmission within the DO-A process. Consequently, this approach is not suitable for the very first D2R transmission of DO-A. Moreover, device-specific R2D transmissions are unavailable at this stage, so they cannot supply the necessary information for the first DO A D2R transmission.<br>- [Huawei] If the necessary information is contained in a device-specific R2D transmission (Option 4), it is necessary for the device to transmit an additional scheduling request which also increases the transmission overhead.<br>- [CATT] Reader cannot know which device would require resource for DO-A transmission. Thus, it is very unlikely that Reader can provide the necessary information by device-specific R2D transmission.<br>- [CMCC] Option 4 is based on RACH procedure ahead, and is not the first D2R transmission for DOA.<br>--- | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600552](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600552.zip) |
| Agreement | On enhancements of D2R TDM(A) for Device 2b and for Device C for CBRA for DT and DO-DTT, capture the following in TR 38.769.<br>---<br>On enhancements to increase the number of time domain resources indicated by the corresponding R2D message for the Access Random ID message (Msg1) to greater than 2, sources (FUTUREWEI, LGE, ETRI, DOCOMO, ASUSTeK, InterDigital, Qualcomm, Samsung, Panasonic, NEC, CATT, Sharp) state that it is feasible. Sources (FUTUREWEI, ETRI, LGE, DOCOMO, ASUSTeK, NEC, Sharp) further state that it is feasible and necessary, and sources (vivo, ZTE, CMCC) state that it is feasible but not necessary. Sources (Huawei, Xiaomi, Ericsson) report that it is not necessary. Following observations were provided during the study.<br>- [FUTUREWEI] mentions that increasing X1 for Msg1 transmission is necessary, as the larger PRDCH overhead can be mitigated by allocating more time‑domain resources.<br>- [LGE] notes that the smaller residual SFO of Device 2b/C may enable the use of more than two TDM resources for Msg1. (e.g., X1 > 2).<br>- [ETRI] states that supporting more than two Msg1 time‑domain access occasions (X1 > 2) is feasible and necessary for device 2b and device C.<br>- [DOCOMO] states that supporting X1 > 2 for Msg1 can enhance multiplexing capacity and shorten the overall random‑access procedure.<br>- [ASUSTeK] states that supporting X1 = {1, 2, 4, 8} for Msg1 is reasonable given the larger coverage and improved SFO capability of active devices.<br>- [Samsung] notes that with the improved timing accuracy of Device 2b/C, X1 for Msg1 transmission can be increased (e.g., 4) though it must remain limited due to A‑IoT device constraints.<br>- [InterDigital] states that supporting X1 > 2 for Msg1 is feasible in Rel‑20 because active devices have higher capability and more accurate clocks.<br>- [Qualcomm] states that supporting X1 > 2 for Msg1 is feasible because device 2b/C can use more accurate clocks and calibration to reduce the required time gap between TDMed D2Rs.<br>- [ZTE] notes that increasing X1 is feasible due to smaller SFO and reduced guard time for Device 2b/C, but its necessity remains unclear because a larger X1 introduces multiple drawbacks and therefore requires further evaluation.<br>- [vivo] notes that although X1 > 2 for Msg1 is feasible due to the reduced SFO in Rel‑20, its necessity is low because the resulting latency‑reduction gain is very limited.<br>- [Huawei] mentions that larger values of X1 for Msg1 are unnecessary because the performance gain is limited and further decreases as X1 increase.<br>- [Xiaomi] notes that it is not necessary to support X1 > 2 for Msg1, as the 16 Msg1 transmission resources enabled by 8 FDM + 2 TDM D2R in Rel‑19 are already sufficient.<br>- [CATT] notes that the maximum number of time domain resources for Msg1 transmission should be limited to 11 for Device 2b/C, as larger values provide limited performance gain.<br>- [CMCC] notes that supporting X1 > 2 for Msg1 can be studied only if Rel-19 Random access procedure is reused.<br>- [Panasonic] notes that supporting X1 > 2 for Msg1 transmissions could be considered due to the lower SFO of Device 2b/C.<br>- [Apple] states that configurable X1 values such as 2, 4, and 8 help reduce Msg1 collision probability in high‑density environments.<br>- [Ericsson] mentions that it should be determined whether X1 = 2 is sufficient for outdoor capacity and latency requirements, and that any need for enhancement beyond X1 = 2 for Msg1 should be justified with supporting evaluations.<br>- [Panasonic] states that the Device types 2b and C have more accurate oscillators with lower SFO. To reduce the signaling overhead for Msg1 trigger signal and Msg2, larger transmission occasions could be considered, i.e., X1>2 and X3>1.<br>- [NEC] states that X1 values such as 4 and 8 to provide more efficient access.<br>On enhancements to increase the number of time domain resources indicated by the corresponding R2D message for the D2R Upper Layer Data Transfer message (Msg3) to greater than 1, sources (FUTUREWEI, LGE, ETRI, DOCOMO, ASUSTeK, Samsung, InterDigital, Qualcomm, vivo, CMCC, Panasonic, NEC) state that it is feasible. Sources (FUTUREWEI, LGE, ETRI, DOCOMO, ASUSTeK) further state that it is feasible and necessary, and source (vivo) states that it is feasible but not necessary. Source (Huawei) reports that it is not necessary, source (Xiaomi) states that it is not feasible, and source (ZTE) states that is neither feasible nor necessary. Following observations were provided during the study.<br>- [FUTUREWEI] mentions that supporting X3 > 1 is feasible due to the higher clock accuracy of Device 2b/C, although increasing X3 beyond 2 is unlikely because of Msg3 length and clock drift.<br>- [LGE] notes that the smaller residual SFO of Device 2b/C may enable the use of multiple TDM resources for Msg3 (e.g., X3 > 1).<br>- [ETRI] states that supporting more than one Msg3 time‑domain access occasion (X3 > 1) is feasible and necessary for device 2b and device C.<br>- [DOCOMO] states that supporting X3 > 1 for Msg3 can improve random‑access efficiency and reduce total access time.<br>- [ASUSTeK] states that enhancements on X3 > 1 should be limited to X3 = 2, since supporting higher X3 adds design and signaling complexity without clear benefit.<br>- [Samsung] notes that the X3 capacity for Msg3 transmission should match that of Msg1 and can likewise be increased (e.g., 4) rather than being restricted to half as in Rel‑19.<br>- [InterDigital] states that supporting X3 > 1 for Msg3 is feasible in Rel‑20 with shorter gaps between time resources compared to Rel‑19.<br>- [Qualcomm] states that supporting X3 > 1 for Msg3 is feasible since improved clock accuracy of device 2b/C makes the time‑gap overhead marginal even for longer Msg3 durations.<br>- [vivo] notes that although X3 > 1 for Msg3 is feasible, its necessity is low given the similarly limited latency‑reduction benefit.<br>- [Huawei] mentions that larger values of X3 for Msg3 are unnecessary because the performance gain is limited and further decreases as X3 increase.<br>- [Xiaomi] notes that it is not feasible to support X3 > 1 for Msg3, as the device ID can be several hundred bits long.<br>- [ZTE] notes that supporting X3 > 1 is technically feasible but brings significant performance and scheduling penalties, so its necessity is weak and requires further study.<br>- [CATT] notes that TDMA support for Msg3 can reduce transmission latency, though the latency‑reduction benefit diminishes as the TDMA size increases, and at least X3 = 2 should be supported for Device 2b/C.<br>- [CMCC] notes that supporting X3 > 1 for Msg3 can be studied.<br>- [Panasonic] notes that supporting X3 > 1 for Msg3 transmissions could also be considered given the improved oscillator accuracy of Device 2b/C.<br>- [Apple] states that configurable X3 values greater than 1 allow parallel Msg3 processing and improve random‑access performance.<br>- [Ericsson] mentions that from the device perspective, X3 should be 1.<br>- [Panasonic] states that the Device types 2b and C have more accurate oscillators with lower SFO. To reduce the signaling overhead for Msg1 trigger signal and Msg2, larger transmission occasions could be considered, i.e., X1>2 and X3>1.<br>- [NEC] states that X3 values such as 2 and 4 to provide more efficient access.<br>--- | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600552](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600552.zip) |

| Agreement | - For Level A/B, at least the following examples of assistance information are identified as needed to assist the further processing at Sensing Function. The assistance information may or may not be provided by RAN.<br>  - Level A<br>    - Location of TRP<br>    - Step size in delay domain if samples in delay domain is reported<br>    - Time information related to OFDM symbols used for measurement<br>    - Antenna configuration and/or beam information<br>  - Level B<br>    - Location of TRP<br>    - LCS to GCS transformation information<br>    - Information related to sampling of delay, doppler and/or angle profile<br>- Additional assistance information is not precluded<br>- No more discussion on the assistance information on Level A/B from RAN to SF is expected in the study item from RAN1 perspective | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | The payload size estimation of each measurement level/option for NR ISAC can be done by the following formulas<br>- Level A: Msubcarrier* Ncpi *Mtxport*Nrxport*Ntrp*(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>Where,<br>  - Msubcarrier: number of subcarriers in a OFDM symbols<br>  - Ncpi: number of OFDM symbols within CPI<br>  - Mtxport: number of reference signal antenna port for sensing purpose<br>  - Nrxport: number of Rx antenna ports<br>  - Ntrp: number of TRPs for payload size calculation<br>  - Nquan1: number of quantized bits for amplitude<br>  - Nquan2: number of quantized bits for angle<br>- Level B:<br>  - Delay-Doppler Profile: Mdelay *Ndoppler *Mtxport* Nrxport *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>  - Delay-angle Profile: Mdelay * Ncpi *Mtxport*Nangle *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>  - Delay-Doppler-angle Profile: Mdelay *Ndoppler *Mtxport*Nangle *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>  - Delay Profile: Mdelay *Ncpi*Mtxport*Nrxport *Ntrp *(Nquan1+ Nquan2)<br>Where,<br>  - Mdelay: number of delay samples in the window<br>  - Ndoppler: number of Doppler samples in the window<br>  - Nangle: number of angle samples in the window<br>- Level C:<br>  - C1/C2/C3: Np1*Ntrp *<br>  - C4/C5: Np2*Ntrp *<br>Where,<br>  - Np1 is total number of paths<br>  - Np2 is total number of points<br>  - Nparam is 5 including e.g.,<br>    - C1/C3/C4: delay, Doppler, horizontal angle, vertical angle, power measurement<br>    - C2/C5: position (x, y, z), radial velocity, power measurement<br>  - Nquan,i: number of quantized bits<br>  - Ntrp: number of TRPs for payload size calculation<br>- Level D:<br>  - Nobject*Ngnb * for options D1/2<br>  - Mpoints* Ngnb * for options D3/4<br>Where,<br>  - Nobject is total number of targets<br>  - Mpoints is total number of scattering points of all targets<br>  - Ngnb is fixed to 1 | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | - The following assumptions on the parameters can be used to estimate payload size of different Levels/Options of measurements for NR ISAC for calculation purpose<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \| Relation to maximum payload calculation \|<br>\| Nsubcarriers \| 3276 for 100MHz, 30kHz SCS \|  \|<br>\| Ncpi \| Min 64, medium 160, max 320 symbols per CPI \|  \|<br>\| Mtxport \| 1, medium 4, max 9 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| Nrxport \| 64 \|  \|<br>\| Ntrp \| 3 \|  \|<br>\| Ngnb \| 1 \|  \|<br>\| Nquan1, i.e., amplitude \| Min 16, medium 16, max 32 \|  \|<br>\| Nquan2, i.e., phase \| Min 16, medium 16, max 32 \|  \|<br>\|  \| Min 16, medium 16, max 32 \|  \|<br>\| Mdelay \| Min 288 (CP length, UMa) Medium/max: 467 for 2*570m (UMa) Max: 1436 for 2*1732m (RMa) \|  \|<br>\| Ndoppler \| same as Ncpi \|  \|<br>\| Nangle \| B2/B3: 32, 360 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions Note: 32 assumes non-uniform sampling Note: 360 assumes uniform oversampling \|<br>\| Np \| 30, 60, 120 \| to calculate min/medium/max for Level-C Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| Nobject for D1/D2 \| 15 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>\| Mpoints for D3/D4 \| 20 \| Note: value obtained based on evaluation assumptions \|<br>  - For Level C<br>    - Nparam=5 parameters for measurements<br>  - For Level D<br>    - Nparam=7 parameters for measurements<br>    - for D2/D3/D4, one more parameter of target ID is assumed<br>Note: for B2/B3, minimum value and maximum value can be different between non-uniform sampling case and uniform oversampling case<br>Note: RAN1 does not require all the parameters to take the minimum values at the same time or the maximum values at the same time<br>Note: above does not take into account the potential impact from measurement quantization on the sensing performance.<br>Note: above does not take into account the potential impact of different Levels/Options on the sensing performance. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |
| Agreement | The following Text Proposal to Conclusion section in TR 38.765 is endorsed.<br>\| The study in physical layer has identified a number of Levels/Options for measurement report as described in Clause 5. Different Levels/Options for measurement report are associated with different payload overhead, assistance information and other higher layer aspects. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601384](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601384.zip) |

| Agreement | For the evaluation of GNSS resilient NR-NTN operation at least for initial access, the companies are encouraged to further study the following candidate solutions<br>- Solution 2D: UE side time/frequency pre-compensation based on reference location or TA/Doppler compensation information provided by gNB.<br>- Solution 1D: Signalling enhancements for Msg2/Msg4 (e.g. enhanced TA command, frequency adjustment command, reference point adjustment command).<br>- Solution 2E: service link time/frequency UE side pre-compensation based on last acquired GNSS position<br>- Solution 1A: Multiple PRACH transmissions (e.g. with different roots or cyclic shifts or different formats or with different time/frequency pre-compensation using multiple reference locations within the uncertainty area) using existing PRACH formats<br>- Solution 2A: Single/multi-satellite DL-TDOA based on current specifications.<br>- Solution 3: Implementation-based techniques e.g. using a long enough PRACH processing window and multiple timing hypotheses for PRACH preamble reception with large max differential delay.<br>- Solution 2B: Multiple random access attempts based on different time/frequency pre-compensation hypotheses (e.g. based on multiple reference points within the uncertainty area)<br>- Solution 1G: Adaptation of PRACH configuration.<br>- Solution 2C: Solutions based on broadcasting DL timestamp(s).<br>To ensure a good consolidation of the results, companies are encouraged to continue evaluating each solution and their combinations in more detail, in particular with respect to the specification impact where relevant (including whether the solutions remain within the scope of the SID), performance, applicability to different scenarios, complexity, coexistence with legacy UEs, and signalling overhead. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600252](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600252.zip) |

9 Release 20 NR › 9.7 Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN) for Internet of Things (IoT) Phase 4

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For R20 NB IoT NTN, at least one UL SPS configuration and at least one DL SPS configuration are supported.<br>- FFS whether to support one additional UL SPS configuration and one additional DL SPS configuration<br>- FFS whether and how to update the transmission parameter(s) (e.g., TBS, MCS, resource assignment, number of repetitions) for a given SPS configuration. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601520](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601520.zip) |
| Agreement | For SPS for R20 NB IoT NTN, explicit activation and release indications are supported.<br>- Regarding the indication for SPS release, down-select one of the following options.<br>      - Option1. DCI<br>      - Option2. MAC CE<br>- Regarding the indication for SPS activation, down-select one of the following options.<br>      - Option1. DCI<br>      - Option2. MAC CE | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601520](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601520.zip) |
| Agreement | For SPS NPDSCH transmission, the legacy behavior (i.e., postponement) is reused when it is collided with the following:<br>- NPSS/NSSS/NPBCH/SIB1-NB/SI on the anchor carrier<br>- Fully reserved DL subframe<br>- Subframes that are not NB IoT DL subframes<br>FFS whether and how to handle the case where a postponed SPS NPDSCH transmission is overlapped with another SPS NPDSCH/SPS NPUSCH transmission/resource.<br>FFS whether and how to handle the case where a SPS NPDSCH transmission is overlapped with a DL transmission gap (i.e., the gap determined by dl-GapDurationCoeff and dl-GapPeriodicity if configured) | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601521](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601521.zip) |
| Agreement | For SPS NPUSCH transmission, the legacy behavior (i.e., postponement) is reused when it is collided with the following:<br>- NPRACH<br>- UL Gap (i.e., 40ms gap after 256 ms UL transmission)<br>- Fully reserved UL subframe<br>FFS whether and how to handle the case where a postponed SPS NPUSCH transmission is overlapped with another SPS NPDSCH/NPUSCH transmission/resource. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601522](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601522.zip) |
| Agreement | For Rel-20 NB-IoT NTN, at least disabling HARQ feedback for SPS NPDSCH transmission without a corresponding NPDCCH is supported<br>- FFS on enabling HARQ feedback<br>- Note: whether HARQ resource indicator in a DCI (if DCI based activation/release is supported) can be used for DCI based HARQ feedback disabling will be discussed separately. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601522](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601522.zip) |
| Agreement | For SPS activation/release indication, RAN1 targets to complete the down-selection between DCI and MAC CE by RAN1#124bis, taking into account at least the following aspects:<br>- Capacity for SPS, e.g., the maximum number of simultaneous voice calls that can be supported on one non-anchor carrier<br>  - Companies’ assumption about SPS periodicity length, DL SPS configuration, UL SPS configuration in the capacity analysis should be provided.<br>- Whether USS is overlapped with SPS transmission/reception occasion(s) or not<br>- Payload size and content of the indication for SPS activation/release<br>- Reduction of data payload that can be carried in a SPS transmission/reception occasion, if any, and potential impact on link-level performance, if any<br>- Coverage performance<br>- Miss-detection of the indication for SPS activation/release<br>- Potential Specification impact<br>- Note: Consider the following related agreement from RAN2:<br>  - RAN2 assumes that dynamic scheduling can also work when SPS is activated | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601523](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601523.zip) |

| Agreement | Following metrics are used for SLS evaluations for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies.<br>- User perceived throughput (UPT), including:<br>  - High percentile (90%)<br>  - mean<br>  - median<br>  - cell edge (5 & 10-percentile)<br>- Optional for full buffer traffic only: cell average throughput<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the CDF of instantaneous UL TX power across all UEs<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the statistics on the UL TX rank.<br>- Companies are encouraged to report the statistics on the applied MCS. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | Following metrics are used for LLS evaluation for multi-layer UL DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM studies.<br>- BLER curves (for a subset of NR MCS (covering whole range with spanning), HARQ re-transmissions disabled) for same transmission rank, for same resource allocation and same transmission power for DFT-s-OFDM and CP-OFDM<br>  - Companies may derive Link-level throughput vs SNR based on BLER curves<br>- Netgain | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2600788](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2600788.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |

| Agreement | For the base graph of LDPC extension beyond NR range, the following is identified in RAN1#124 for further study<br>- The LDPC extension is quasi-cyclic LDPC codes, the parity check matrix of quasi-cyclic LDPC Codes is defined at least by a matrix H of size (mb×z)×(nb×z), which consists of sub-block matrices of size z×z, where each sub-block matrix is composed by circularly shifted matrices or zero matrices. Wherein, mb, nb and z are integers larger than 1.<br>- The values of mb, nb and z are FFS.<br>- The row weight of circularly shifted matrices is less than or equal to 2.<br>- FFS: Whether row weight of all circularly shifted matrices is 1, or the row weight of some circularly shifted matrices is 1 and the row weight of some circularly shifted matrices is 2. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601666](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601666.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |

## 10.3.2 Modulation, Joint channel coding and modulation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.3 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| Agreement | - Parameters affect the PS complexity/storage/latency and BLER and throughput performance trade-off are at least:<br>- DM output length in unit of I/Q symbol,<br>- # of bits shaped per I/Q,<br>- # of shaping parameters<br>- # of DM blocks needed to support the target throughput of 6GR<br>- DM algorithm and bit-width of variables in the DM algorithm<br>- Impact of mismatch on the quantization bitwidth between DM and DDM.<br>- Parameters affect the GS complexity/storage/latency and BLER and throughput performance trade-off are at least:<br>- 1D-NUC or 2D-NUC<br>- Constellations and  # of constellations<br>- Bitwidth for describing the constellation<br>- Bits to constellation mapping<br>- The values of these parameters used in evaluations shall be submitted together with performance results.<br>- Companies are encouraged to provide evaluations for different combination parameters to study different performance and complexity/storage/latency trade-offs to provide proper assessment including feasibility . | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

## 10.5.1.1 Synchronization acquisition and beam measurement

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For initial access and mobility in 6GR, study the following deployment scenarios<br>- Single beam and multi-beam based deployments<br>- Single TRP and multi-TRP based deployments<br>- Single carrier and multi-carrier deployments<br>- Other deployment scenarios | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | For 6GR paging transmission/reception, study at least the following aspects:<br>- Study paging transmission scheme(s) to facilitate network energy savings<br>- Study paging scheme(s) to facilitate UE energy savings<br>- Study necessity of paging capacity enhancement<br>- Study necessity of paging coverage enhancement | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Study 6GR signals, channels and procedures for initial access and idle mobility, considering at least<br>- Cell/Initial Cell search and cell ID identification<br>- Time/frequency synchronization/tracking<br>- Beam measurement<br>- System information acquisition<br>- Whether TRP is transparent/non-transparent to UE during above procedures<br>Study 6GR signals, channels and procedures for idle mobility, considering at least<br>- Cell search and cell ID identification<br>- Time/frequency synchronization/tracking<br>- Beam measurement<br>- System information acquisition<br>- Whether TRP is transparent/non-transparent to UE during above procedures | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | For 6GR measurements in initial access and for mobility, study measurement resource, measurement quantity, measurement functionality and measurement procedure, at least including:<br>- L1 measurements<br>- Cell-level/[TRP-level] and beam-level measurement | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Study beam operation during 6GR initial access, including:<br>- Beam acquisition during initial access<br>- Beam report/refinement during initial access<br>- Feasibility and performance of spatial/temporal beam prediction during initial access | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |
| Agreement | Basic 6GR sync signal structure is defined as 6GR SSB, which consists of primary synchronization signal(s), secondary synchronization signal(s) and physical broadcast channel(s)<br>- FFS: Other types of 6GR sync signal/channel structure or reference signal and their structure | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx) |

## 10.5.1.2 PRACH and RACH procedure

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Study random access framework with the following aspects:<br>- Enablement of energy efficient random access procedures (supporting SID objective 1b);<br>  - Including both network and UE power saving<br>- Coverage improvement (supporting SID objective 1d);<br>- Support of random access for diverse device types and capabilities (supporting SID objective 1g);<br>- System performance improvement from overhead reduction, simplification of signaling/configurations (supporting SID objective 1k);<br>- Additionally consider following aspects<br>  - random access latency;<br>  - capacity<br>  - detection reliability;<br>  - high speed mobility;<br>- Note: Other aspects identified during future discussions are not excluded.<br>The following scenarios and assumptions beyond single carrier/TRP are considered for the study of above random access framework:<br>- NTN<br>- SBFD<br>- multi-carrier<br>- multi-TRP<br>- Note: whether/how to support one or more of the scenarios/assumptions, including whether any special handling or functionality needs to be introduced in support of the scenarios/assumptions is part of the study. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-PRACH]` |

## 10.5.1.3 Bandwidth operation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system › 10.5.1 Initial access and mobility

| Agreement | Adopt the following simulation assumptions for tracking RS evaluation<br>Table 1: LLS assumptions for T/F tracking<br>\| Parameter \| Value \|<br>\| Duplex, Waveform \| FDD and TDD, CP-OFDM \|<br>\| Carrier Frequency/SCS \| 700MHz/15kHz, 4GHz/30kHz, 7GHz/30kHz, 30GHz/120kHz \|<br>\| Number of TRPs \| NTRP=1, 2, 4 Companies should report the transmission assumptions for the RS transmission for tracking in case of more than 1 TRP. \|<br>\| Bandwidth for PDSCH \| 5MHz, 20MHz, 100MHz, bandwidth for tracking RS can be reported by companies \|<br>\| MIMO scheme \| SU-MIMO, Rank 1,2,3,4 \|<br>\| Modulation \| QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, 256QAM and 1024QAM \|<br>\| Precoding granularity \| PRG = wideband, other value(TBD) \|<br>\| Channel Model \| CDL channel in TR38.901 For CDL-C for 4, 7 and 30 GHz - with combination of ASA and ASD scaling values in sec. 7.7.5.1 in TR 38.901, for above 6 GHz cases - ZSA = 5°, ZSD = 1° The CDL table is translated so that the strongest cluster’s AoD and AoA occur at a random angle for both the antenna panels of TRP and UE in the local coordinate. The value of the random angle is selected to be uniformly distributed from +30 to -30 degree. The random value is chosen independently for both AoD and AoA. In mTRP cases, the channel is generated per TRP. \|<br>\| Delay spread \| 30ns, 100ns, 300ns, 1000ns \|<br>\| UE speed \| 3km/h, 10km/h, 120km/h, 350km/h 500km/h \|<br>\| Initial time offset (TO) \| 1/X CP, X= 8 Other values can be reported by companies. \|<br>\| CFO \| Initial acquisition TRP: uniform distribution +/- 0.05 ppm UE: uniform distribution +/- 5, 10, 20ppm (each company to choose one) Non-initial acquisition Per TRP: uniform distribution +/- 0.05 ppm UE: uniform distribution [+/- 0.1] ppm Note 1: Those parameters are used for simulation assumptions for synchronization signals/channels in NR (TR38.802). (dH, dV) = (0.5, 0.5)λ Around 7GHz carrier frequency 128 TXRUs/728AEs: (M, N, P, Mg, Ng, Mp, Np) = (24,16,2, 1, 1, 4,16). Companies can report the CE methods. \|<br>\| Performance metric \| Estimation error (e.g., CFO/Dopler rmse), BLER, Throughput \| | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other Aspects related to CSI]` |

| Agreement | In 6GR, DL and UL HARQ operation designs considers at least the following aspects:<br>  - latency<br>  - reliability<br>  - coverage<br>  - power saving (NW and UE)<br>  - NW complexity<br>  - UE complexity<br>  - diverse services/applications/traffics<br>  - system efficiency/system throughput/user throughput<br>  - feedback efficiency/UL and DL overhead<br>Note: the design of DL and UL HARQ does not necessarily be the same | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For DL HARQ in 6GR, study both following HARQ-ACK feedback mechanisms<br>- Mechanism 1: HARQ-ACK information bits are transmitted via L1 signalling<br>- Mechanism 2: HARQ-ACK information bits are transmitted via higher layer signalling (e.g., MAC CE) | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | For discussion purposes,<br>- Asynchronous HARQ refers to that retransmission(s) occurs in a non pre-determined occasion once the corresponding initial transmission is scheduled.<br>- Adaptive HARQ refers that the transmission parameters and resources for the retransmission can be adaptively adjusted.<br>- For DL and UL in 6GR, support asynchronous and adaptive HARQ operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | Study possible HARQ-ACK payload size range. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |
| Agreement | In 6GR, support at least TB level granularity for HARQ-ACK feedback | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-HARQ related]` |

## 10.5.5 Other physical layer signals, channels and procedure

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.5 Multi-antenna system

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Study the UE-to-UE cross-link interference for 6GR:<br>- Study and identify the applicable scenarios of UE-to-UE cross-link interference.<br>- Study the characteristics of UE-to-UE cross-link interference, for example what is the potential interference signal level, what is the potential impact on system operation.<br>- Study the mechanisms and techniques for handling UE-to-UE cross-link interference, including UE-to-UE cross-link interference measurement and reporting<br>  - For each candidate mechanism, evaluate and analyze the performance benefit, impact to the system and complexity at BS/UE.<br>  - Study the candidate resource or signals/channels for measuring UE-to-UE cross-link interference.<br>  - Study and identify the candidate measurement quantities.<br>  - Study the reporting mechanisms | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study BS-to-BS cross-link interference for 6GR:<br>- Study and identify the applicable scenarios of BS-to-BS cross-link interference.<br>- Study the characteristics of BS-to-BS cross-link interference, for example what is the potential interference signal level, what is the potential impact to the system operation.<br>- Study the mechanisms and techniques that can handle the BS-to-BS cross-link interference, including the mechanisms for measuring and identifying the BS-to-BS cross-link interference:<br>  - For each considered mechanism, evaluate the performance benefit, impact to the system operation, and complexity at BS/UE.<br>  - The candidate resource for measuring BS-to-BS cross-link interference.<br>  - The measurement quantities at least for evaluation purpose. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | For handling remote interference in 6GR:<br>- Study the applicable scenarios for remote interference between remote cells due to atmospheric ducting;<br>- Study the impact of remote interference to the system, including the impact to the uplink reception.<br>- Study the characteristics of the remote interference.<br>- Study the candidate mechanisms for measuring/detecting/identifying remote interference, mechanisms to mitigate remote interference.<br>  - For each candidate mechanism, evaluate the benefits and impact to the system.<br>Note: UE reporting to request uplink resource scheduling is to be discussed under this agenda | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |
| Agreement | Study the UE reporting mechanism for requesting uplink resource with considering at least the following aspects:<br>- Applicable use cases, at least including requesting resource for UL data transmission<br>- UL transmission latency<br>- Signalling overhead<br>- UL resource efficiency<br>- System capacity<br>- Network/UE complexity<br>- other aspects are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Other signals, channels, and procedures]` |

## 10.6.1.2 WUS operation in RRC states

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation › 10.6.1 Downlink WUS and operation

|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Study 6GR DL WUS triggering PDCCH monitoring with and without C-DRX in RRC connected state, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>- Impact to UPT, if applicable<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Impact to QoS/delay budget satisfaction rate, if applicable<br>- Network overhead/complexity<br>- Network energy consumption<br>- Other impacts, if any | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |
| Agreement | For RRC idle state, study serving cell RRM measurement based on 6GR measurement signal (e.g., at least 6GR sync signal) by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>  - measurement based on EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring vs measurement based on non-EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring<br>- coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>For RRC idle state, study neighboring cell RRM measurement based on 6GR measurement signal (e.g., at least 6GR sync signal) by EE processing, considering at least:<br>- UE energy saving gain<br>  - measurement based on EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring vs measurement based on non-EE processing together with DL-WUS monitoring<br>- Neighboring cell identification, measurement and evaluation<br>- Neighboring cell number limitation, if any<br>- Inter-cell interference<br>- Coverage (e.g., achievable SINR/SNR) and accuracy<br>- Impact on the EE processing complexity<br>- FFS the power consumption of neighboring cell RRM measurement based on EE processing<br>Note: It doesn’t mean the measurement in EE processing has to be coupled with DL WUS monitoring | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-WUS operation in RRC states]` |

## 10.6.2 Uplink WUS and operation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.6 WUS and operation

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Study further necessity and feasibility of UL WUS under the following deployment scenario (DS):<br>- DS#1: Standalone cell where UE obtains UL WUS configuration e.g., from standalone cell and/or pre-defined in the specifications<br>  - DS#1a: Prior to UL WUS, nothing transmitted on the standalone cell<br>  - DS#1b: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with or without PBCH on the standalone cell but no SIB1 on the standalone cell<br>  - DS#1c: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on the standalone cell and periodic SIB1 on the standalone cell<br>  - Note: UL WUS is transmitted to the standalone cell<br>- RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Coverage target of UL WUS<br>  - How UE acquires synchronization for UL WUS<br>  - How UE decides UL WUS transmission power<br>  - RRC states for the above scenarios<br>- Note: The above scenarios can be applicable to multi-TRP deployment scenario if TRP is transparent to UE at the time of UL WUS transmission<br>- Note: The above scenarios are for study purposes only and do not imply 6GR support. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |
| Agreement | Study further necessity and feasibility of UL WUS under the following deployment scenario (DS):<br>- DS#2: Multi-cell/carrier where UE obtains UL WUS configuration from cell/carrier #1<br>  - Always-on 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #1, and SIB1 on cell/carrier #1<br>  - DS#2a: Prior to UL WUS, nothing transmitted on cell/carrier #2<br>  - DS#2b: Prior to UL WUS, always-on 6GR synchronization signals with or without PBCH on cell/carrier #2 but no SIB1 on cell/carrier #2<br>  - DS#2c: Prior to UL WUS, 6GR synchronization signals with PBCH on cell/carrier #2 and periodic SIB1 on cell/carrier #2<br>  - Note: UL WUS can be transmitted to cell/carrier #1 or cell/carrier #2<br>- RAN1 to consider at least<br>  - Coverage target of UL WUS<br>  - How UE acquires synchronization for UL WUS<br>  - How UE decides UL WUS transmission power<br>  - Whether the cells/carriers are in the same band or not<br>  - Whether the cells/carriers are collocated or not<br>  - RRC states for the above scenarios<br>  - Whether the above scenarios can be applicable to multi-TRP deployment scenario<br>- Note: The above scenarios are for study purposes only and do not imply 6GR support. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-Uplink WUS]` |

## 10.7.1 NTN specific requirements and design for GNSS based operation

10 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 10.7 NTN

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For NTN link budget template, RAN1 to take the TN link budget template as baseline with specific rows / values (including adding new rows) to be further discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | RAN1 will define evaluation parameters for at least the following combinations of satellite orbit and bands:<br>- S-band:<br>  - LEO 300, LEO 600, GEO<br>- Ka band:<br>  - [LEO 300], LEO 600, [LEO 1200], GEO<br>- Ku band:<br>  - LEO 1200, GEO<br>NOTE 1: The evaluations for S band are expected to be similar to L-band.<br>NOTE 2: This is only for the purpose of evaluations. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | RAN1 to use the following terminology when discussing GNSS availability at least for physical layer operation.<br>- GNSS-based: Refers to the network mode of operation which relies on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver and the devices can obtain a position fix within a given accuracy<br>  - FFS: How often the UE may be required to obtain a position fix, which may be related to the required accuracy.<br>  - FFS: if position under this operation can be obtained by means other than GNSS that provides a comparable accuracy, e.g. pre-configuration for a fixed device. This may also include information other than positioning.<br>- GNSS-degraded: Refers to the network mode of operation which relies on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver, the devices were able to obtain a position fix at some point in time, but the devices may not currently have a position fix within a given accuracy.<br>  - NOTE: The UE may be able to use the position fix for physical layer operation<br>- GNSS-free/GNSS-less: Refers to the network mode of operation which does not rely on devices being equipped with a GNSS receiver, or devices are equipped with a GNSS receiver but do not have a current position fix that can be used for physical layer operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601469](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601469.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | 6GR NTN targets to support GNSS-based operation, GNSS-degraded operation and GNSS-less/GNSS-free operation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | 6GR NTN uplink time-frequency synchronization follows the same principle as NR NTN as baseline:<br>- The concept of “uplink synchronization reference point” is introduced in 6GR NTN.<br>- 6GR NTN provides satellite assistance information<br>- At least for GNSS-based operation, it is supported that UE uses its own location information + satellite assistance information to perform time-frequency pre-compensation. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Conclusion | As a general principle for 6GR NTN study:<br>- Under NTN agenda item, we will identify issues / requirements specific to NTN.<br>- Potential solutions to these issues / requirements may be studied under the NTN agenda<br>  - The outcome of this study may be discussed under other agenda items if common design is possible.<br>  - These solutions may end up resulting in an extension of the TN design.<br>  - This may depend on the solution / issue / requirement<br>- NTN specific solutions may be introduced when a common / extended design cannot meet the NTN requirements.<br>- When targeting a common design TN performance is prioritized. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601470](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601470.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | 6GR supports large scheduling offsets to accommodate the RTT introduced by the satellite channel. Further discuss how to realize these scheduling offsets:<br>- Option 1: Reuse the k_offset concept from NR as a baseline with potential modifications.<br>  - NOTE: Under this option, additional scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR) may be supported for scheduling flexibility, which apply in addition to k_offset.<br>- Option 2: The large scheduling delays to accommodate the RTT are incorporated into the scheduling offsets (e.g. similar to K1/K2 in NR), which may or may not be common for TN and NTN | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Conclusion | When reporting inputs for the link budget template and evaluation assumptions to RAN1#124b, companies are encouraged to provide them in an xls attached to their contribution following the format of the xls attached to R1-2601471. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
| Agreement | Study NTN specific issues/requirements at least for the following aspects for 6GR NTN under this agenda:<br>- Uplink time-frequency synchronization<br>- HARQ issues: e.g. enable/disable HARQ feedback (which may include how to efficiently operate with HARQ feedback enabled/disabled), number of HARQ processes, etc.<br>- Timing relationships with large RTT.<br>- Coverage target<br>- Physical layer aspects of multi-satellite operation (including multi-orbit)<br>- Aspects related to Multiple beams per satellite<br>- Aspects related to satellite having more beam footprints than simultaneously active beams<br>Other aspects are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#124 - eom_03](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23124%20-%20eom_03.docx)<br>[R1-2601471](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_124/Docs/R1-2601471.zip)<br>`[124-R20-6GR-NTN specific]` |
